==============================================================================
BareSnap v2.4.0 - INCREMENTAL BACKUP WITH DEDUPLICATION AND NATIVE SSH SUPPORT
Pure C11 Engine + yad GUI (shell) + ncurses TUI. Static binary, zero dependencies.
==============================================================================
"Serious backups for people who don't want to rely on anything."

Language:   C11 (engine and TUI), Shell (yad GUI)
Binary:     Static CLI ~2 MB (x86_64 with AES-NI) / ~1.0 MB (ARM64/aarch64),
            TUI included (WARNING TUI IS BIT BUGGY!!)
Format:     FORMAT_VERSION=1, compatible with original C++ engine
Crypto:     Dual-crypto: XChaCha20-Poly1305 + AES-256-GCM (selected at init)
Hash:       Dynamic Hybrid: FNV1A (Lightweight Legacy) + BLAKE2B_128 (Cryptographic)
Status:     Production. 935/935 regression tests (FNV1A + BLAKE2B_128, meaning 
            1870 tests) passing (including full SSH suite, rare routes battery, 
            CLI edge cases, integrated final boss, extreme resilience battery, 
            chaos engineering, dual-crypto AES-256-GCM, DeLorean Effect, 
            Y2K38 Apocalypse, Bit Rot, ENOSPC, Concurrent Swarm, Zombie Inode, 
            Pack Tamper, Config Fuzzing, Battery Death, Entropy Firewall, 
            Dynamic Snapshot Compression, Health without false positives, 
            Index Grow anti-OOM, Hot CRC32C append, LRU Evacuation, 
            LIST/readdir battery, integrated Valgrind modules and regression 
            of the 4 structural fixes v2.3.5: F1, O1, M1, R1).
            Valgrind: 0 leaks / 0 errors across all modules.
            ASan/UBSan: 58/58 tests, zero memory errors.
Revision:   Manual revised for v2.4.0:


Download progress in extract SSH, hardlinks and memory fixes,
SSH timeout in initial handshake, mutual exclusion in create,
new global --pass-fd flag, documented RAM defenses and
battery expanded to 935 regression tests.


- Dual-crypto: --encrypt aes activates AES-256-GCM with AES-NI.
- Delorean Effect: immune to CMOS stack reset (1970 epoch).
- Y2K38 Apocalypse: 64-bit time_t survives beyond 2039.
- Induced Bit Rot: GCM Tag detects 1-byte mutation.
- ENOSPC: atomic rollback without zombie temporaries.
- Concurrent Swarm: lock file serializes 5 processes.
- Zombie Inode: hot file deletion during CDC.
- Pack Tamper: physical mutation detected by GCM/CRC.
- Config Fuzzing: corrupt config blocks verify and create.
- Battery Death: kill -9 midway through create + repair.
- Health without false positives: invalid key aborts cleanly
  without moving healthy snapshots to damaged/ during --repair.

- anti-OOM Index Grow: re-hashing without secondary load factor.
- hot append CRC32C: exact absorption of appended bytes.
- LRU Evacuation: global byte counter + anti-dangling memset.
- robust LIST: snprintf validation in agent handle_list.
- Protocol V3: opcodes migrated to 0x40-0x5F (no v2 collision).
- Frankenstein Repo: config+index deleted; create survives with controlled
  auto-init when index metadata does not exist.

- Inconsistent Repo: config manually deleted with live index; create
  aborts with "inconsistent state" and health --repair recovers the repo.

- health --repair: purges zombie temporaries tmp/*.tmp and recreates lost
  or unreadable config when safe to do so.

- Entropy Firewall: microsecond abort of ZSTD/LZ4 on white noise.
- Dynamic Compression: native LZ4/ZSTD coexistence in the same physical pool.
- VFS Sanitization: purging of zombie virtual SSH network contexts.
- 935 regression tests (variable time measured on Dual Core).
- FIX v2.3.2: Robust ID parsing in handle_sync_index (strtoull + errno).
- FIX v2.3.2: Elimination of -Wformat-truncation warnings with oversized
  buffers in remote agent and CLI.

- FIX v2.3.2: Deterministic compilation with real build date.

==============================================================================
PORTABILITY, ARM64 AND "REAL METAL" (SUPPORTED AND UNSUPPORTED PLATFORMS)
==============================================================================

BareSnap is not a desktop toy; it is an infrastructure tool.
Platform support is governed by a single rule: absolute freedom over
silicon, respect for the POSIX standard, and zero submission to closed ecosystems.

GNU/LINUX (x86_64 / aarch64):

Sovereign territory. From a 2007 Core 2 Duo to a modern Threadripper.
ARM64 (Raspberry Pi 3/4/5, SBCs, ARM NAS):

The engine includes `compile_arm64.sh`, which downloads the musl.cc
cross-toolchain and forges a ~999 KB static binary. Requires neither SIMD nor AES-NI;
pure C cryptographic fallback (XChaCha20) guarantees it works on the
first try. Validated under `qemu-aarch64-static` emulation on a 2007 Core 2 Duo
in 0.5 seconds. If it compiles and runs emulated in hell, it flies natively
on a Raspberry Pi.

HAIKU OS (The BeOS legacy):
BareSnap is Linux-only by design and survival decision. Any experimental support
for Haiku OS/BeOS, macOS, or Windows is officially discarded.
Maintaining exotic polyfills and #ifdef conditionals for desktop systems
only weakens engine resilience and exposes RAM security (mlock,
getrandom). Real Metal demands 100% focus on the penguin. End of story.

macOS / iOS (EXPLICITLY REJECTED):
Due to SDK licensing issues, the tyranny of Xcode, and Apple's monopolistic nonsense,
macOS is NOT supported nor ever will be. BareSnap requires raw kernel access,
`getrandom`, `utimensat`, and `/dev/tty` without a corporate intermediary
asking us for permission, charging us a developer fee, or limiting our compiler.
Real Metal is free; if your silicon is hijacked by Cupertino, look for another tool.

WINDOWS / MSYS2 / CYGWIN (THE CEMETERY OF ATTEMPTS):
We tried. Truly. We took the code, put it into MSYS2/MinGW, and
started patching.
Result: `flock` does not exist natively (you have to fight with `LockFileEx`),
`getrandom` is a joke, `utimensat` is conspicuously absent, and Windows'
threads/POSIX model is a mockery of the C11 standard.

To make it work on Windows, one would have to create an abstraction layer of
4,000 lines of messy code (`#ifdef _WIN32` everywhere) and depend
on DLLs or heavy runtimes.

That goes against the project's religion. BareSnap will not get its hands dirty
with the Windows registry, nor with the lack of a real POSIX VFS.

If you use Windows for your backup server, the problem is not BareSnap,
it's your infrastructure. Use WSL2 or, better yet, switch to Linux.

==============================================================================
DEVELOPMENT AND TESTING ENVIRONMENT
==============================================================================

All code and the 935-test battery have been developed and validated
on the following hardware, without access to modern machines during the process:

CPU:     Intel Core 2 Duo T7500 @ 2.201 GHz, 4GB RAM (2 cores, year 2007)
OS:      Linux Mint 21 x86_64 (kernel 5.15 LTS)
RAM:     Limited (laptop of the era)
Disk:    Mechanical HDD (no SSD)
Toolchain: musl-gcc, gcc, ncurses 6.5, zstd, xdelta3, monocypher, AES-256-GCM
           (AES-NI) + Argon2

Key results on this hardware:

- 935/935 regression tests, (FNV1A + BLAKE2B_128 so 1870 tests)
- 2.15 GB incremental backup (107,826 files) in 0.872 seconds
- SSH incremental backup (2953 files) in 0.354 seconds (12.2x faster)
- `baresnap info` query with hot cache: 38 milliseconds
- Real deduplication ratio: 71.89x (2.15 GB -> 30.67 MB)

"If it works here, it works on anything."

NOTE: All times and ratios mentioned in this document have been
measured in the environment described above. On modern hardware (NVMe SSD,
CPU with AVX2, 8+ cores), results are orders of magnitude better.

==============================================================================
QUICK START
==============================================================================

Compile everything:
    ./compile.sh

Run CLI:
    ./build/baresnap --help
    ./build/baresnap init /mnt/backup/repo
    ./build/baresnap init /mnt/backup/repo --compression zstd --zstd-level 10
    ./build/baresnap init /mnt/backup/repo --encrypt
    ./build/baresnap create /mnt/backup/repo ~/projects

Remote backup via SSH (agent installs automatically):
    ./build/baresnap remote install ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo
    ./build/baresnap create ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo ~/projects

Remote backup with password (without SSH keys):
    ./build/baresnap remote install ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo
    ./build/baresnap create ssh://user@server:/path/to/repo ~/projects
    # Will prompt for password interactively via internal askpass.
    # For one-off automation: BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD="key" ./build/baresnap ...

Run GUI (requires yad installed on the system):
    ./gui/baresnap-gui.sh

Run TUI (native terminal interface):
    ./build/baresnap tui
    ./build/baresnap tui /mnt/backup/repo ~/projects

Regression tests:
    BARESNAP=./build/baresnap ./mega_test.sh
    BARESNAP=./build/baresnap ./mega_test.sh --no-ssh  # If no SSH is configured

Practical one-off example to create a backup with AES and ZSTD, remember
that the two executables (baresnap and baresnap-remote) must be kept together at 
least the first time

1-Install the remote:

./baresnap remote install ssh://test@192.168.1.2/home/test/notepad-plus-plus-8.9.7 

Note: The example folder is created automatically by the program

Creating remote directory...
password: 
Copying baresnap-remote (/home/test/baresnap_v2_2_2/baresnap-remote)
password: 
Setting executable permissions...
password: 
Remote binary checksum OK
OK: baresnap-remote installed at /home/test/notepad-plus-plus-8.9.7/.local/bin/baresnap-remote

2-Configure which encryption we want: chacha20 (default) or aes (if desired), 
the compression type (default is lz4 WITHOUT level) or zstd with level 10

Example with aes and zstd level 10

./baresnap create ssh://test@192.168.1.2/home/test/notepad-plus-plus-8.9.7 
--encrypt aes --compression zstd --zstd-level 10 

password:  
New passphrase: (enter the password for encryption here, write it down or if 
you lose it you will no longer be able to access)
Confirm passphrase: 

Repository initialized at 'ssh://test192.168.1.2/home/testt/notepad-plus-plus-8.9.7'
Encryption: AES-256-GCM + Argon2id
Compression: ZSTD (level 10)
[SSH] 8 RPCs, 3.342s total, 417.7ms avg | Sent: 160 B, Recv: 0 B

3-Perform the backup and that's it!

./baresnap create ssh://test@192.168.1.2/home/test/notepad-plus-plus-8.9.7 ../../notepad-plus-plus-8.9.7/
password:  (from ssh)
Passphrase: (from backup encryption)
scan      / ...                                                                                     
SSH Optimization: loading full remote index into RAM...
SSH: 0 segments (0 cache, 0 downloaded)
warning: remote index segments not loaded; using blooms
pack      - ...                                                                                     
created snapshot: ssh://test@192.168.1.2/home/test/notepad-plus-plus-8.9.7/snapshots/2026-09-01_01-58-49-424_1f951042.snap
entries: 2673
cache: 0 hits, 2402 misses

[TIMING] create total: 18.340s (it is normal for it to take a little while the first time and more if you enter passwords 
manually)

[SSH] 35 RPCs, 3.923s total, 112.1ms avg | Sent: 14.98 MB, Recv: 160 B

Perform backup with ssh and non-interactive password:

BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD="test" ./build/baresnap create ssh://test@192.168.1.2/home/test/baresnap ~/docs

==============================================================================
1. WHAT IS BARESNAP
==============================================================================

BareSnap is an incremental backup tool with content-based
deduplication. It was rewritten from C++ to pure C11 while maintaining
total binary compatibility with FORMAT_VERSION=1.

Main features:

- Content-defined chunking: FastCDC (16 KB min, 64 KB avg, 256 KB max)
- Deduplication: BLAKE2B-128 hash per chunk with global index
- Compression: LZ4 (default) or ZSTD (level 1-22) per chunk
- Encryption: XChaCha20-Poly1305 with Argon2 KDF (64 MB, 3 passes)
- Delta encoding: xdelta3 for files up to 256 KB (or 256 MB
  with --delta-binary) ONLY works in local mode, disabled in SSH

- On-disk Bloom Filter: lazy loading of index for large repos
- Integrity: CRC32C per pack and FNV1A-64 per snapshot
- Hardlinks and symlinks preserved by (dev, ino)
- Metadata: uid, gid, mode, mtime_ns, ctime_ns
- Auto-init: create initializes repo if it doesn't exist
- Concurrent prune: pack rewriting with 2 local workers
- Native SSH backend: automatically deployable lightweight agent
- SSH authentication: public keys AND interactive password
- Local SSH index cache: 12.2x faster in incremental backups
- extract automatically sanitizes paths with trailing or redundant slashes

The final CLI binary is static and dependency-free at runtime.
Size varies by architecture due to cryptographic acceleration paths:

- x86_64: ~2 MB (includes AES-NI + PCLMULQDQ intrinsics for Turbo Silicon).
- ARM64:  ~1.0 MB (uses pure C fallback, more compact as it lacks SIMD).

The GUI is a shell script over yad (native GTK dialogs) and the TUI
is integrated into the same CLI binary.

==============================================================================
2. ENGINE ARCHITECTURE
==============================================================================

The engine is organized into C11 modules with clear responsibilities.
+-------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| FILE                    | RESPONSIBILITY                                           |
+-------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| baresnap-remote.c       | Remote agent: binary protocol over stdin/stdout          |
| brs_buffer.c            | Dynamic buffers, little-endian reader                    |
| brs_cache.c             | File cache for incrementals with self-healing            |
| brs_chunker.c           | FastCDC streaming                                        |
| brs_config.c            | Config read/write (includes cipher_algo)                 |
| brs_crypto.c            | Dual crypto: XChaCha20/AES-256-GCM (AES-NI) + Argon2     |
| brs_delta.c             | xdelta3 wrapper                                          |
| brs_dir.c               | Directory listing                                        |
| brs_fsutil.c            | Filesystem operations                                    |
| brs_hash.c              | FNV1A-128 / XXH3-128 / BLAKE2B-128                       |
| brs_index.c             | Global index + Bloom Filters + lazy loading              |
| brs_init.c              | Repo init (UUID, ZSTD) and cipher_algo selection         |
| brs_lock.c              | Mutual exclusion (locks) between concurrent processes    |
| brs_lz4.c               | LZ4 compression                                          |
| brs_manifest.c          | Source scanning + snapshots (parse with                  |
|                         | centralized cleanup goto parse_error, FIX M1)            |
| brs_pack.c              | Pack read/write                                          |
| brs_pipeline.h          | Pipeline definitions and structures                      |
| brs_pool.c              | Buffer pool (anti-OOM)                                   |
| brs_remote.c            | Remote protocol client                                   |
| brs_remote_test.c       | Remote agent unit and integration tests                  |
| brs_remote_v3.h         | Remote protocol v3 (sync_index, upload_pack)             |
| brs_repo.h              | Stable public API                                        |
| brs_repo_common.c       | progress, cancel, derive_key, lazy index, reading        |
| brs_repo_create.c       | create pipeline, WorkerCtx/EmitCtx, workers              |
| brs_repo_health.c       | Anomaly detection and repair                             |
| brs_repo_internal.h     | Internal header: shared types and helpers                |
| brs_repo_lock.h         | Constants and definitions for lock file                  |
| brs_repo_ls.c           | list, ls, search and formatting                          |
| brs_repo_prune.c        | retention, snapshot order and concurrent prune           |
| brs_repo_query.c        | info, diff and load_key                                  |
| brs_repo_restore.c      | full restore + selective extract                         |
| brs_repo_verify.c       | verify: CRC32C, hashes, compression, encryption, delta   |
| brs_spsc.c              | Producer/consumer SPSC queue                             |
| brs_tui.c               | Midnight Commander style ncurses TUI                     |
| brs_types.h             | Base types, constants, magics, flags                     |
| brs_uri.c               | URI parsing for SSH backend                              |
| brs_util.c              | Utilities: RNG, time, I/O, KVV, fnv1a                    |
| brs_vfs.h               | Abstract filesystem interface (VFS)                      |
| brs_vfs_context.c       | Active VFS context management                            |
| brs_vfs_dispatch.c      | VFS Dispatcher: routes calls to local or SSH             |
| brs_vfs_local.c         | VFS backend for POSIX local filesystem                   |
| brs_vfs_ssh.c           | SSH VFS backend: agent, askpass, RPC counter, write-back |
| brs_zstd.c              | ZSTD compression (libzstd wrapper)                       |
| main.c                  | Main CLI + internal askpass + TUI entry point            |
+-------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+

FIX M1 (v2.3.5) — brs_manifest.c / brs_parse_snapshot():
The snapshot parser was refactored to centralize ALL error paths
into a single parse_error label. Previously, each of the ~40 early return -1
could leak previously allocated memory (hostname, root_path, entries array
and their chunks/delta_source). Now, upon any failure:

1) The decrypted buffer is wiped (sensitive crypto plaintext wiped).
2) All heap allocated so far is freed (hostname, root_path,
   entries and their chunks and delta_source_chunks arrays).
3) The snapshot is left in a deterministic zero state, so the caller
   can safely and idempotently invoke brs_parsed_snapshot_free().

Associated test: section 109 of mega_test (7 tests, with Valgrind validation).

==============================================================================
2.0A LOGICAL ORDER OF THE PROCESSING PIPELINE (CREATE)
==============================================================================

To optimize the deduplication ratio and CPU usage, the data transformation
flow of a chunk follows a strictly linear order based on information entropy:

Block Reading (FastCDC) -> Delta Calculation (XDelta3) -> Compression
(ZSTD/LZ4) -> Dual Encryption (XChaCha20 / AES-256-GCM) -> Pack Writer

Technical Justification: Compression algorithms like ZSTD destroy local
repetitive patterns by generating high entropy outputs. Executing
XDelta3 on pre-compressed blocks nullifies the delta engine's capacity
to find common matches. Processing the binary delta before compression
guarantees the minimum storage signature in incremental revisions of
modified files.

Metadata flow: pack -> index + bloom -> snapshot
Guarantee: the snapshot is written AT THE END; a power outage never leaves a
snapshot pointing to incomplete data.

v2.3.8 — Pack accumulation in the global index

Context:
    Only affected SSH.
    Locally, since remote rotation does not exist, the index was already generated properly.

During pack rotation, brs_pack_writer_rotate finalizes the current pack
and retrieves its entries.

Those entries are inserted into the global BrsIndexMap (new_index) via
writer.index_map.

When the pipeline finishes, a single index segment is written with all
chunks from all packs.

writer.index_map is preserved when reinitializing the writer with memset.

This fixes:
    - MISSING chunks
    - pack 0 for chunk...
    - loss of references in SSH backups larger than 750 MB

==============================================================================
2.0B SSH HANDSHAKE DIAGRAM (REMOTE VFS)
==============================================================================

CLIENT (baresnap)                      REMOTE SERVER
==================                     ================
brs_vfs_ssh_open(uri)
        |
        +---> fork() + execlp("ssh", "-T", ...)
        |        |
        |        |   ssh user@host
        |        |   "cd /repo && exec
        |        |    ./.local/bin/baresnap-remote --repo ."
        |        |         |
        |        |         v
        |        |   +-----------------------+
        |        |   |  baresnap-remote      |
        |        |   |     (agent)           |
        |        |   |                       |
        |        |   |  stdin <--- pipe_wr   | (client writes)
        |        |   |  stdout ---> pipe_rd  | (client reads)
        |        |   +-----------------------+
        |        |
        v        v
+----------------------------------+
|       BINARY PROTOCOL (6-byte header)    |
|                                                  |
|   type(1) | flags(1) | length(4 LE) | payload    |
|                                                  |
|   MSG -->  OPEN  CLOSE  READ   WRITE  RENAME     |
|            UNLINK LIST  STAT   HARDLINK MKDIR BYE|
|                                                  |
|   RSP <--  OK    DATA   HANDLE ENTRIES STAT ERROR|
+----------------------------------+
        |
        +---> poll(pipe_rd, 2 s)
        |     POLLIN  --> agent responded  --> continue
        |     POLLHUP --> agent died       --> error
        |     timeout --> agent waiting    --> continue
        |
        v
VFS Operations:
    fopen / fread / fwrite / rename / unlink / mkdir / ...
    each call = 1 RPC (request + response)
    global mutex protects each atomic RPC

LOCAL INDEX CACHE (v2.1.4):
+-----------------------------------------------+
| ~/.cache/baresnap/<hash>/index/               |
|   1787768297791881676.idx                     |
|   1787768639656186268.idx                     |
|   ...                                         |
+-----------------------------------------------+

.idx segments are cached locally after the first download.
Subsequent incremental backups load indices from local disk
(0 RPCs) instead of downloading them from remote.

AUTHENTICATION:
+-----------------------------------------------+
| Public key:     ssh-keygen + ssh-copy-id      |
| Password:       internal binary askpass       |
|                 (--askpass-internal)          |
|                 reads from /dev/tty with termios|
| Automation:     BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD=...     |
+-----------------------------------------------+

v2.3.8 — Pack upload by remote agent with ACK

Reason:
    The previous SSH streaming corrupted large packs.
    Locally this problem did not exist.
    Failures appeared in SSH with backups of ~750 MB or more.

Pack uploading no longer uses external scp or VFS write-back.

Flow:

1. Client sends BRS_MSG_UPLOAD_PACK.
2. Client reads local temporary pack in 4 MiB blocks.
3. Each block is sent as BRS_MSG_DATA.
4. Remote agent writes with pwrite and replies BRS_RSP_OK.
5. Client waits for ACK before the next block.
6. Agent does fsync and replies with total size.
7. Client verifies remote size matches local size.

This eliminates:
    - corruption due to desynchronization
    - happy uploads
    - external dependencies like scp

==============================================================================
2.1 ENGINE MODULARIZATION (REFACTOR PERFORMED)
==============================================================================

The old monolith src/brs_repo.c (>3,500 lines) was split into 1 internal
header and 7 .c modules, keeping brs_repo.h's public API intact.

Current modules:

- brs_repo_internal.h: shared internal header (not public).
- brs_repo_common.c: progress, cancel, derive_key, lazy index, chunks.
- brs_repo_create.c: create pipeline, workers, hardlinks, split_sources.
- brs_repo_restore.c: full restore and selective extract.
- brs_repo_verify.c: integrity verification (CRC32C, hash, delta).
- brs_repo_prune.c: retention, snapshot order and concurrent prune.
- brs_repo_query.c: info, diff and load_key.
- brs_repo_ls.c: list, ls, search and formatting.

Refactoring notes:

- brs_repo.h did not change; the public API remains stable.
- compile.sh links the 7 .c modules instead of the old brs_repo.c.
- The original file was kept as brs_repo.c.bak for safety.
- Regression tests passed without changes after modularization.

==============================================================================
2.0C CONCURRENCY DIAGRAM (MULTITHREADED PIPELINE)
==============================================================================

The engine uses a producer/consumer model based on lock-free SPSC
(Single-Producer Single-Consumer) queues to maximize performance on
multicore CPUs without mutex contention on the hot path.

[ DISK READER / SSH VFS ]  <-- Main Thread
        |
        | (FastCDC streaming)
        v
+-------------+
|  Chunker    |  --> Divides files into 16KB-256KB chunks
+-------------+
        |
        | (brs_spsc_queue_t)  [Lock-free, cache-line aligned]
        v
+---------------------+       +---------------------+
|  Worker 0 (CPU)     |       |  Worker 1 (CPU)     |
|  - Delta (xdelta3)  |       |  - Delta (xdelta3)  |
|  - Compression      |       |  - Compression      |
|    (LZ4 / ZSTD)     |       |    (LZ4 / ZSTD)     |
|  - Encryption       |       |  - Encryption       |
|    (XChaCha20/GCM)  |       |    (XChaCha20/GCM)  |
+---------------------+       +---------------------+
        |                           |
        | (Processed chunks)        |
        +------------+--------------+
                     |
                     v
                  [ SPSC Queue ]
                     |
                     v
        +------------------+
        |   Pack Writer    |  <-- Writer Thread (I/O Bound)
        | (4MB Grouping)   |      - Local: write() to /tmp
        | (Batching)       |      - SSH: 4MB buffer + mass upload
        +------------------+
                     |
                     v
        [ Index / Bloom / Snapshot ]  <-- Final atomic update

DESIGN NOTES:

- SPSC queues (brs_spsc.c) avoid mutexes between Chunker and
  Workers, and between Workers and Pack Writer.

- The global SSH mutex (h->ssh->mtx) is ONLY acquired inside the
  fread/fwrite loop of the Pack Writer, protecting the atomic RPC
  and preventing starvation of local workers.

- The Pack Writer groups chunks until reaching 4 MB (write-back batching)
  before releasing the SPSC queue and executing I/O.

- BRS_NUM_WORKERS = 2 (local). In SSH, prune operates with 1 worker
  to maintain binary protocol stability over the pipe.

==============================================================================
[ASCII DIAGRAM] DETAILED MULTITHREADED PIPELINE FLOW (CREATE)
==============================================================================

  +----------------------------------+
  | MAIN THREAD                      |
  | - Source scanning (readdir)      |
  | - Inode cache validation         |
  +----------------------------------+
                   |
                   | (Sequential reading of disk blocks / SSH VFS)
                   v
  +----------------------------------+
  | CHUNKER (FastCDC Streaming)      |
  | - Dynamic chunking (16K-256K)    |
  +----------------------------------+
                   |
                   | Puts tasks into queue (buffer_slot)
                   v
      ============================
      LOCK-FREE SPSC QUEUE (Chunk)
      ============================
         /                      \
        / (Internal distribution)\
       v                          v
+-----------------------+  +-----------------------+
|  WORKER 0 (CPU Thread)|  |  WORKER 1 (CPU Thread)|
|                       |  |                       |
| 1. Entropy            |  | 1. Entropy            |
|    Firewall           |  |    Firewall           |
| 2. Delta (xdelta3)*   |  | 2. Delta (xdelta3)*   |
| 3. Compression        |  | 3. Compression        |
|    (LZ4 / ZSTD)       |  |    (LZ4 / ZSTD)       |
| 4. Dual Encryption    |  | 4. Dual Encryption    |
|    (ChaCha / AES-GCM) |  |    (ChaCha / AES-GCM) |
+-----------------------+  +-----------------------+
       \                          /
        \ Put processed chunks   /
         v                      v
      ============================
      LOCK-FREE SPSC QUEUE (Emit) 
      ============================
                   |
                   v
  +----------------------------------+
  | PACK WRITER (I/O Bound Thread)   |
  | - Grouping into 4 MB blocks      |
  | - Local: write() to /tmp         |
  | - SSH: Upload + ACK per block    |
  +----------------------------------+
                   |
                   | (Upon successful completion of all packs)
                   v
  +----------------------------------+
  | FINAL ATOMIC UPDATE              |
  | - Writing Index (.idx)           |
  | - Bloom Filter (.blm)            |
  | - Final Snapshot (.snap)         |
  +----------------------------------+

*Note: Delta encoding injected before compression. Disabled in SSH by default.


==============================================================================
2.2 I/O RETRY IN RESTORE (TRANSIENT FAULT TOLERANCE)
==============================================================================

Automatic retry was added for transient I/O errors during pack reading,
designed for slow USB drives, NAS, or storage returning occasional EIO.

Implementation:

- Scope: pack reads in read_chunk_from_pack().
- Protected calls: open()/openat() and pread()/pread64().
- Retried errors: EIO and EAGAIN.
- Maximum retries: 3.
- Exponential backoff: 50 ms, 100 ms, and 200 ms.
- Destination writes are not blindly retried to avoid masking corruption
  or duplicating data after a partial failure.

Behavior:

- If retry succeeds, operation continues normally.
- If limit is exhausted, chunk is marked as error and the affected file
  is left unrestored.

==============================================================================
2.3 SSH AUTHENTICATION WITH PASSWORD (INTERNAL ASKPASS)
==============================================================================

BareSnap supports SSH PASSWORD authentication in addition to public keys.
This is implemented via an internal askpass helper integrated directly
into the binary, without external dependencies.

Askpass architecture:
1. main.c generates an executable wrapper at:
   ~/.local/libexec/baresnap-askpass
2. The wrapper calls:
   /absolute/path/baresnap --askpass-internal "$@"
3. The --askpass-internal mode is a FIREWALL: it runs BEFORE any
   ncurses/TUI initialization. It is a pure CLI mode.
4. The password is read from /dev/tty using termios (without system("stty")),
   with echo disabled and guaranteed restoration.
5. For one-off automation (tests), the password can be injected via
   environment variable:
   BARESNAP_SSH_PASSWORD="my_key"

Complete flow:
    baresnap create ssh://user@host/repo ~/data
      -> brs_vfs_ssh_open() launches ssh with SSH_ASKPASS=wrapper
      -> ssh needs password -> executes wrapper
      -> wrapper executes baresnap --askpass-internal "Password: "
      -> --askpass-internal opens /dev/tty, reads password without echo
      -> password is written to stdout -> ssh receives it
      -> authentication completed -> remote agent starts
      -> binary protocol over pipes

Note: --askpass-internal mode does NOT initialize ncurses or TUI. It is a pure
CLI mode that only reads a password and terminates.

Note on TUI and Askpass:
To ensure the askpass script can read the password from /dev/tty without
ncurses interface interference, the TUI executes endwin() (temporarily exiting
curses mode) right before launching the worker thread.

This restores the TTY to cooked mode. Furthermore, the askpass script now forces
'stty cooked icanon' to be 100% compatible with minimal POSIX shells
like dash (used in Debian/Mint).

==============================================================================
2.4 AGENT SUDDEN DEATH MANAGEMENT (ANTI-DEADLOCK MECHANISM)
==============================================================================

The synchronous binary communication channel over SSH between the local client
and the remote agent (baresnap-remote) is protected against indefinite thread
blocks (application-level deadlocks) in case of catastrophic failure, system panic,
or child process interruption.

Control Mechanism (native poll): Read operations on the file descriptor (fd)
of the SSH stdout pipe are strictly governed by Linux's poll() system call.
Direct blocking synchronous reads (read()) without prior verification of data
availability are prohibited.

Timeout Policy: Initial handshake and pipe availability are verified with
a 2-second poll(). If the remote agent suffers a segmentation fault or an abort
mid-transfer, the local client breaks the wait cycle, releases descriptors,
atomically marks the current snapshot as failed/incomplete, and aborts cleanly,
avoiding left-hanging background processes.

============================================================================
2.5 THREAD-LOCAL PACK CACHE (SSH PERFORMANCE OPTIMIZATION)
============================================================================

Problem:
In restore and extract operations on SSH remote repositories,
each chunk required 3 binary RPCs over the SSH pipe:
1. OPEN  (open pack)
2. READ  (read chunk)
3. CLOSE (close pack)

Solution:
A thread-local cache was implemented that maintains the complete content
of each pack in RAM. The first time a chunk from a pack is needed,
the entire pack is read (1 STAT RPC + 1 READ RPC) and stored in a
thread-local buffer. Subsequent reads of any chunk from the same pack
are resolved with a memcpy from RAM, without any additional RPCs.

Parameters:
PACK_CACHE_SIZE           16 slots per thread (covers 16 simultaneous packs)
Maximum size per pack:    256 MB (anti-OOM safety limit)
Total byte limit:         256 MB per thread (PACK_CACHE_MAX_BYTES)
Eviction:                 Real LRU based on access timestamp
                          (monotonic tl_pack_cache_clock)
Cache lifetime:           Per thread; freed with brs_pack_cache_flush()

FIX v2.3.1 — LRU Eviction with global counter:
The tl_pack_cache_bytes counter tracks total cached bytes per thread.
When inserting a new pack would exceed PACK_CACHE_MAX_BYTES, the entry
with the lowest last_access (real LRU) is evicted until there is space.
This eliminates OOM from accumulation of 256 MB packs × 16 slots × 2 threads.

FIX v2.3.0 — Strict anti-dangling-pointer cleanup:
In pack_cache_free_entry(), after free(data):
1. data = NULL and data_size = 0 (immediate disarming).
2. Array is compacted (swap with last slot).
3. memset(&tl_pack_cache[last], 0, sizeof(...)) is executed on
   the freed slot. This guarantees no dangling pointers survive
   on the thread stack.

Flush points:
brs_pack_cache_flush() is invoked at the end of:

- brs_repo_restore()   (pass_end: point)
- brs_repo_extract()   (cleanup: point)
- create_worker()      (before return NULL)

Measured impact (Core 2 Duo T7500, local SSH):
Without cache:  7.1 seconds (3300 chunks, ~9900 RPCs)
With cache:     4.4 seconds (3300 chunks, ~14 RPCs + transfer)
Compatibility:

- Cache is only activated when an active VFS context (SSH) is present.
- Local repos use direct pread() without cache.
- Cache is thread-local (__thread), so there is no contention
  between workers or need for additional mutexes.

==============================================================================
2.6 ELIMINACIÓN DEL HEALTH CHECK DE RUTAS CALIENTES
==============================================================================

Create, restore, and extract operations do not execute a health check at startup.
Repository integrity is verified via:

- The independent command "baresnap health [repo] [--repair]"
- The "baresnap verify" command (full verification)
- The BARESNAP_SKIP_HEALTH=1 variable continues to be respected
  for test script compatibility.

Justification:
The health check is a diagnostic operation that should not be executed
on every data operation. Production scripts (cron) should run
"baresnap health" or "baresnap verify" as a separate step,
not as part of backup/restore.

==============================================================================
2.7 OPTIMIZATION OF THE INFO COMMAND
==============================================================================

info gets the index entry count in memory
(brs_index_map_count) instead of parsing each pack individually.
Pack sizes are obtained via brs_vfs_stat() (1 RPC per pack)
instead of brs_read_file() (full download).

Impact:
Before: info = ~3.6 seconds (24 MB download)
Now: info = ~0.03 seconds (metadata only)

==============================================================================
2.8 FILE CACHE SELF-HEALING (CACHE HEALING)
==============================================================================

The repository 'cache' file can become corrupted or bloated after
multiple prune operations or power outages, accumulating orphan
or desynchronized entries.

Solution:
A hot failure detector was implemented inside the create pipeline.
If find_previous_version() fails consistently (threshold of 5
consecutive failures), the engine assumes the cache is corrupted.

Automatic behavior:
1. Deletes the physical file repo/cache.
2. Reinitializes the in-memory structure to empty.
3. Emits a clear warning: "[WARNING] File cache inconsistency detected..."
4. Continues backup by regenerating a clean cache from scratch.

Impact:

- Eliminates need for manual maintenance (rm repo/cache).
- Guarantees delta encoding works again automatically.
- First backup after healing is slower (re-indexing), but
  subsequent ones recover optimal incremental speed.

============================================================================
2.9 SSH LOCAL INDEX CACHE (NEW IN V2.1.4)
============================================================================

.idx segments are cached locally at:
    ~/.cache/baresnap/<repo_hash>/index/
Where <repo_hash> is an FNV1A-64 hash of the repository URI.

Flow:
1. First execution: downloads all .idx files from remote and saves them
   to local cache.
2. Subsequent executions: checks if .idx exists in local cache.
   If it exists, loads it from local disk (0 RPCs). If it does not exist
   (new segment), downloads it from remote (1 RPC) and saves it.

Implementation:

- Function: ssh_load_all_indexes() in brs_repo_create.c
- Cache dir: ~/.cache/baresnap/<hash>/index/
- Hash: FNV1A-64 of repo URI to isolate caches per repo
- Verification: brs_path_exists() before downloading
- Local load: brs_load_index_segment_from_file()

Measured impact (Core 2 Duo T7500, SSH with 180ms RTT):
Without local cache:  4.3 seconds (38 index download RPCs)
With local cache:     0.35 seconds (0 RPCs, load from local disk)
Improvement: 12.2x faster in incremental backups

==============================================================================
2.10 DISABLING DELTA ENCODING OVER SSH (NEW IN V2.1.4)
==============================================================================

Delta encoding requires reconstructing the previous file version
by reading chunks from remote packs. Over SSH, this generates hundreds of RPCs.

Solution:
When an active SSH VFS context is present, delta encoding is automatically
disabled. The engine does normal chunking of the new file instead
of calculating deltas.

Implementation:
In brs_repo_create.c, inside the task loop:
    if (brs_vfs_context_get() != NULL) {
        delta_max = 0;  // Disable delta over SSH
    }

Justification:
Delta encoding is a space optimization, not a time one. Over
SSH, the latency cost of reconstructing previous versions exceeds
the space savings.

==============================================================================
2.11 OPTIMIZED WRITE-BACK BATCHING (NEW IN V2.1.4)
==============================================================================

Write-back batching limits maximum write size to 4 MB per RPC.

Implementation:
In brs_vfs_ssh.c, ssh_max_write() function:
    if (m > (4u * 1024u * 1024u))
        m = (4u * 1024u * 1024u);

Impact:

- 20 MB pack: from 20 RPCs to 5 RPCs
- Savings: ~2.4 seconds (15 RPCs × 160ms)
- No memory impact (buffer already existed, just used more)

==============================================================================
2.12 LOCAL TEMPORARY PACK + MASS UPLOAD (NEW IN V2.1.4)
==============================================================================

When an active SSH VFS context is present, the pack is written first to a local
temporary file (/tmp/baresnap_pack_<id>.tmp), and upon completion uploaded
to remote in a single mass write.

Implementation:
In brs_pack.c:
1. brs_pack_writer_init(): detects remote VFS, creates temp in /tmp
2. brs_pack_writer_add_chunk(): writes to local file (0 RPCs)
3. brs_pack_writer_finalize(): reads complete pack, uploads it with
   brs_vfs_fwrite() in a single mass RPC

Flow:
Local:  open(/tmp/pack.tmp) -> write × 100 -> close -> rename
SSH:    open(/tmp/pack.tmp) -> write × 100 (local) -> close
        -> read complete -> mass write (1 RPC) -> unlink local
Impact:

- 64 KB pack with 1 chunk: from 3 RPCs to 1 RPC
- 6 MB pack with 100 chunks: from 103 RPCs to 4 RPCs
- Savings: ~15.8 seconds on large packs

==============================================================================
2.13 ELIMINATION OF REDUNDANT FSYNCS (NEW IN V2.1.4)
==============================================================================

Fsyncs over SSH are now "lazy" by default. They do not perform a real flush,
the flush occurs in fclose().

Implementation:
In brs_vfs_ssh.c, brs_vfs_ssh_fsync() function:
    int brs_vfs_ssh_fsync(BrsVfsFile *f)
    {
        /* By default we don't flush on fsync to avoid breaking batching.
           Real flush occurs in fclose() and before read().
           Strict mode: BRS_SSH_STRICT_FSYNC=1 */
        if (ssh_lazy_fsync())
            return 0;
        return ssh_flush_write(h);
    }

To force strict fsync:
    BRS_SSH_STRICT_FSYNC=1 ./build/baresnap create ssh://...

Impact:

- Eliminates redundant RPCs in metadata writes
- Integrity is not compromised: remote agent performs real fsync
  when closing the handle

==============================================================================
2.14 SSH RPCS DIAGNOSIS (NEW IN V2.1.4)
==============================================================================

Global RPC counters with accumulated timing, and debug mode to
view each individual RPC.

Implementation:
Global counters in brs_vfs_ssh.c:
    static uint64_t g_ssh_rpc_count = 0;
    static uint64_t g_ssh_rpc_ns = 0;

Automatic report when closing connection:
    [SSH] 30 RPCs, 2.487s total, 82.9ms avg

Debug mode (view each RPC):
    BRS_SSH_DEBUG=1 ./build/baresnap create ssh://...

Output:
    [SSH-RPC] fopen                config
    [SSH-RPC] stat                 config
    [SSH-RPC] fread                -
    [SSH-RPC] fclose               -
    [SSH-RPC] list                 index
    ...

Usage:
To diagnose slow backups over SSH:
    BRS_SSH_DEBUG=1 ./build/baresnap create ssh://repo ~/data 2>&1 | \
        grep "\[SSH"

This displays all RPCs and the final summary, allowing identification
of redundant operations or bottlenecks.


v2.3.8 — Pack progress

During create, clean messages are shown:

    [pack] Creating package 1...
    [pack] Uploading package 1 (5688 chunks, 100.16 MB)

They indicate package number, chunk count, and package size.

==============================================================================
2.15 SSH: KEEPALIVE AND NETWORK STATISTICS (V2.2.2)
==============================================================================

SSH Keepalive:
The SSH client is launched with:
    -o ServerAliveInterval=15
    -o ServerAliveCountMax=3

This sends a keepalive every 15 seconds. If the server does not
respond 3 times in a row (45s), SSH closes cleanly instead
of leaving the pipe hanging. Prevents "cannot finalize pack" in
long creates (>30s of local processing).

Network statistics:
Upon closing each SSH connection, it prints:
    [SSH] 38 RPCs, 3.782s total, 99.5ms avg | Sent: 11.74 MB, Recv: 242.76 KB

g_ssh_bytes_sent / g_ssh_bytes_recv counters are incremented
in ssh_flush_write (buffered path) and in the direct write
path of brs_vfs_ssh_fwrite (piece >= wbuf_cap).

Configurable timeout via CLI (v2.3.6):
The global --timeout <ms> flag replaces the BRS_SSH_TIMEOUT_MS environment variable.
It is accepted in any command-line position, before or after the subcommand:

baresnap create --timeout 300000 ssh://user@host:/repo ~/data  # Satellite (5 min)
baresnap --timeout 60000 create ssh://user@host:/repo ~/data   # Slow WAN (1 min)
baresnap info --timeout 15000 ssh://user@host:/repo             # Fast LAN

Default: 60000 ms (1 minute). If not specified, this value is used.
The parser extracts the flag before command dispatch, so it works
as a true global option for any subcommand.

==============================================================================
2.16 INCONSISTENT REPO DETECTION AND CONFIG RECOVERY (V2.2.2)
==============================================================================

Problem:

- If a user manually deletes the `config` file but leaves `packs/`,
  `snapshots/` and, above all, `index/` alive, the engine MUST NOT
  auto-initialize the repository as if it were new.

- Doing so would destroy the repo identity and could generate a Frankenstein
  state with orphaned snapshots/packs.

- If `config` is missing but index metadata does not exist, the repo can be
  treated as new or as a recoverable Frankenstein.

Behavior in create:
1. `create` attempts to load `config`.

2. If `config` does not exist or is unreadable:

   - If `.idx` index segments or equivalent metadata exist in
     `index/`, it aborts with a clear message:
       error: repository ... is in an inconsistent state

   - If no index exists, controlled auto-init/recreation of the
     repository is allowed.

3. This allows differentiating:

   - New repo / Frankenstein without metadata -> create survives.
   - Inconsistent repo with live metadata -> create aborts.

Behavior in health:

- `health` detects missing or unreadable `config` as an anomaly.
- `health --repair` reconstructs/recreates `config` when safe to do so.
- After repair, `create` works again.

Associated test:

- Test 81: Inconsistent repo (config manually deleted).
  * create detects corruption and aborts.
  * health --repair recovers the repo.
  * create works after repair.

Note on encrypted repos:
If the repository was encrypted and `config` with KVV/salt is lost,
full cryptographic recovery may not be possible. Repair can restore structural
operability of the repo, but it does not replace the original key if lost.

==============================================================================
2.17 PURGING ZOMBIE TEMPORARIES IN HEALTH --REPAIR (V2.2.2)
==============================================================================

A `kill -9`, power outage, or network failure can leave orphan temporary
packs in `tmp/`:
    repo/tmp/pack_*.tmp

These files are not part of any complete snapshot and must be purged
during repair.

Behavior:

- `health --repair` purges `tmp/*.tmp` before reconstructing/repairing.
- This guarantees clean post-mortem audits after:
  * kill -9 during create.
  * kill -9 during prune.
  * ENOSPC.
  * network cuts over SSH.

Associated tests:

- Test 56: Power outage during prune.
- Test 74: Space Apocalypse / ENOSPC.
- Test 79: Battery death during create.


==============================================================================
2.18 COMPILATION SCRIPTS
==============================================================================
BareSnap includes 5 specialized compilation scripts for different
development and deployment lifecycle purposes:

+------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| Script                 | Purpose                                          |
+------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| compile.sh             | Production: optimized static binary              |
| compile_gbd.sh         | Debug: full symbols for GDB/Valgrind             |
| compile_san.sh         | Memory audit: ASan + UBSan                       |
| compile_tsan.sh        | Concurrency audit: ThreadSanitizer               |
| compile_arm64.sh       | Cross-compile ARM64 (Raspberry Pi, SBCs)         |
+------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+

==============================================================================
2.18.1 COMPILE.SH (PRODUCTION)
==============================================================================
Purpose: Generate the final production binary, static and optimized.

Characteristics:
- Static binary ~2 MB (x86_64) or ~1.0 MB (ARM64)
- -O2 optimization
- Automatic detection of AES-NI + PCLMULQDQ
- Anti-telemetry: -ffile-prefix-map removes absolute paths
- Determinism: SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH for reproducible builds
- Full strip: removes debug symbols

Key flags:
  -std=c11 -O2 -static -no-pie
  -ffunction-sections -fdata-sections -Wl,--gc-sections
  -ffile-prefix-map=$(pwd)=.
  -fno-ident -fno-asynchronous-unwind-tables

Usage:
  ./compile.sh

Result:
  build/baresnap (main binary)
  build/baresnap-remote (SSH agent)

==============================================================================
2.18.2 COMPILE_GDB.SH  (DEBUG)
==============================================================================
Purpose: Generate debug binary with full symbols.

Characteristics:
- No optimization (-O0)
- Full symbols (-g -ggdb3)
- Frame pointers (-fno-omit-frame-pointer)
- No inline (-fno-inline)
- Larger binary (~3-4 MB) but fully inspectable

Key flags:
  -O0 -g -ggdb3 -fno-omit-frame-pointer -fno-inline

Usage:
  ./compile_gdb.sh

Result:
  build/baresnap (with debug symbols)
  build/baresnap-remote (with debug symbols)

Use cases:
- Interactive debugging with GDB
- Profiling with perf or gprof
- Valgrind (needs -O0 for accurate reports)

==============================================================================
2.18.3 COMPILE_SAN.SH (MEMORY AUDIT)
==============================================================================
Purpose: Detect memory bugs with sanitizers.

Characteristics:
- AddressSanitizer (ASan): buffer overflows, use-after-free, leaks
- UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer (UBSan): undefined behavior
- No optimization (-O0) for accurate reports
- Not static (links ncurses dynamically)
- Slower binary (~2-3x overhead)

Key flags:
  -g -O0 -fsanitize=address,undefined
  -fno-omit-frame-pointer -fno-optimize-sibling-calls

Usage:
  ./compile_san.sh
  BARESNAP=./build_san/baresnap ./mega_test.sh

Result:
  build_san/baresnap (with ASan/UBSan)
  build_san/baresnap-remote (with ASan/UBSan)

Bug detection:
- Buffer overflows (stack, heap, global)
- Use-after-free
- Memory leaks
- Undefined behavior (signed overflow, null deref, etc.)

NOTE: AES-NI intrinsics are disabled under sanitizers to avoid
interference with assembly code.

==============================================================================
2.18.4 COMPILE_TSAN.SH (CONCURRENCY AUDIT)
==============================================================================
Purpose: Detect data races between threads.

Characteristics:
- ThreadSanitizer (TSan): data race detection
- Minimal optimization (-O1) required to instrument C11 atomics
- Cannot be mixed with ASan/UBSan
- zstd compiled with TSan for full audit
- Not static (links ncurses dynamically)

Key flags:
  -g -O1 -fsanitize=thread -fno-omit-frame-pointer

IMPORTANT: We use -O1 instead of -O0 because TSan needs minimal optimization
to correctly instrument C11 atomics and avoid massive false positives.

Usage:
  ./compile_tsan.sh
  TSAN_OPTIONS='halt_on_error=1:history_size=7' \
  BARESNAP=./build_tsan/baresnap \
  ./mega_test.sh --no-ssh

Result:
  build_tsan/baresnap (with TSan)
  build_tsan/baresnap-remote (with TSan)

TSan options:
  halt_on_error=1    Aborts on first detected data race
  history_size=7     Increases access history for detailed backtraces

Bug detection:
- Data races between threads
- Concurrent access without synchronization
- Incorrect use of C11 atomics

v2.3.4 validation:
  320/320 tests passing under TSan
  0 data races detected
  3 concurrency fixes applied (ssh_delta_enabled, g_brs_ssh_full_index,
  find_previous_version with mutex)

==============================================================================
2.18.5 COMPILE_ARM64.SH (CROSS-COMPILE ARM64)
==============================================================================
Purpose: Generate static binary for ARM64 (Raspberry Pi 3/4/5, SBCs).

Characteristics:
- Automatic download of musl.cc toolchain if it doesn't exist
- Cross-compilation with aarch64-linux-musl-gcc
- Static binary ~1.0 MB
- Pure C cryptographic fallback (no AES-NI, no SIMD)
- Base ARMv8 architecture (-march=armv8-a)

Toolchain:
  https://musl.cc/aarch64-linux-musl-cross.tgz (~100 MB)
  Downloaded and extracted automatically in ~/cross-tools/

Key flags:
  -march=armv8-a
  -static -no-pie
  -ffile-prefix-map=$(pwd)=.

Usage:
  ./compile_arm64.sh

Result:
  build/baresnap (static ARM64)
  build/baresnap-remote (static ARM64)

Verification:
  file build/baresnap
  # Should show: ELF 64-bit LSB executable, ARM aarch64

Validation:

  Validated under qemu-aarch64-static on Core 2 Duo (0.5 seconds)
  "If it compiles and runs emulated in hell, it flies natively on an RPi"

==============================================================================
2.18.6 COMMON PATTERNS IN THE 5 SCRIPTS
==============================================================================
All scripts share these characteristics:

1. PATH ANTI-TELEMETRY:
   MAP_FLAGS="-ffile-prefix-map=$(pwd)=."
   Removes the absolute build directory path from the binary.
   Two builds on different machines produce the same SHA256.

2. ABSOLUTE DETERMINISM:
   export SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH="${SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH:-1788118500}"
   export ZERO_AR_DATE=1
   Freezes the date in the binary for reproducible builds.

3. RESTRICTIVE XDELTA3 DEFINES:
   -DXD3_MAIN=0 -DREGRESSION_TEST=0 -DSECONDARY_DJW=0 ...
   Compiles xdelta3 "naked", without the main binary or tests.
   Result of 100 compilation iterations with musl-gcc.

4. SPARTAN NCURSES:
   --enable-static --disable-shared --disable-widec
   --without-progs --without-tests --without-cxx --without-ada
   Only the minimal library, no widechar, no programs.

5. LINKING WITH --gc-sections:
   -Wl,--gc-sections combined with -ffunction-sections -fdata-sections
   Removes all dead code from static libraries.

==============================================================================
2.18.7 TYPICAL DEVELOPMENT WORKFLOW
==============================================================================
1. Normal development:
   ./compile.sh
   → Production binary ~1.2 MB

2. Debugging:
   ./compile_dbg.sh
   → Debug binary ~3-4 MB
   → GDB, Valgrind, profiling

3. Memory audit:
   ./compile_san.sh
   → Binary with ASan/UBSan ~5-8 MB
   → Run mega_test.sh under sanitizers
   → Detect memory bugs

4. Concurrency audit:
   ./compile_tsan.sh
   → Binary with TSan ~5-8 MB
   → Run mega_test.sh --no-ssh under TSan
   → Detect data races

5. Release for ARM64:
   ./compile_arm64.sh
   → Static ARM64 binary ~1.0 MB
   → Deploy on Raspberry Pi / SBCs

==============================================================================
2.18.8 BINARIES COMPARISON
==============================================================================
+-------------------+----------+----------+----------+----------+----------+
| Script            | Optim.   | Sanitiz. | Static   | Size     | Use      |
+-------------------+----------+----------+----------+----------+----------+
| compile.sh        | -O2      | None     | Yes      | ~1.2 MB  | Prod.    |
| compile_dbg.sh    | -O0      | None     | Yes      | ~3-4 MB  | Debug    |
| compile_san.sh    | -O0      | ASan+UB  | No       | ~5-8 MB  | Audit.M  |
| compile_tsan.sh   | -O1      | TSan     | No       | ~5-8 MB  | Audit.C  |
| compile_arm64.sh  | -O2      | None     | Yes      | ~1.0 MB  | ARM64    |
+-------------------+----------+----------+----------+----------+----------+

NOTE: Sanitizer scripts (san, tsan) are not static because
they require dynamically linking with the sanitizer libraries.

==============================================================================
2.18.10 REAL ZSTD VS LZ4 BENCHMARK TEST
==============================================================================

Empirical sample on hostile hardware (GM965 Chipset / Core 2 Duo mobile). 
Processing 8.73 GB of logical data corresponding to /usr/lib (high density 
of inodes and pre-compiled binaries).

Deduuced physical decision matrix:
1. ZSTD-10 maximizes silicon and mitigates disk I/O stress. It generates 
   a 1.14 GB savings in network compared to LZ4, with the CPU acting as a natural 
   thermal throttle stabilizing the network flow at 4.76 MB/s. RAM consumption 
   rises to 217 MB (RSS 222876) due to dictionary persistence.
   
2. LZ4 frees up the processor execution queue, reducing total time 
   by 23% (from 1832s to 1400s) and scaling real transfer speed 
   to 6.24 MB/s (the structural physical limit of the magnetic platter). The price 
   is a 40% increase in data volume transmitted through VFS and 
   281 additional RPCs over SSH.

Engineering Conclusion: BareSnap v2.4.0 does not require inheriting Cloud 
abstractions of dynamic allocation. The engine allows hot-swapping between 
efficient computational throttling (ZSTD) and pure sequential flow 
(LZ4), absorbing SSH socket latency (1,023 concurrent RPCs) in a 
fully symmetric and deterministic manner.

Every 1,0s: ps -C baresnap -o pi...  mint-VGN-SZ61WN-C: Sun Sep  6 14:12:59 2026

    PID    PPID %MEM   RSS CMD
1093096  473170  5.5 222876 
./baresnap --buffer-size=32M create ssh://testt@192
.168.1.2:/home/test/liblinux /usr/lib/ --compression zstd --zstd-level 10

pack      - ...                                                                                     
created snapshot: ssh://testt@192.168.1.2/home/test/liblinux/snapshots/2026-09-06_04-22-46-445_a7a8ea8a.snap
entries: 87014
cache: 0 hits, 72704 misses
